● Independent verification is complete — here's the cross-check scorecard:

  Agent 1 (GUI scroll/quit) — ✅ All claims confirmed by my own reads, plus two
  additions of mine: the shift-left pushHistory semantics that make the
  60-second onset exact, and the fact that the warmed 15-minute Processes run
  measured only 1.58% CPU — so idle ticks alone can't explain "severe" lag; the
  scroll-frame interaction is load-bearing.

  Agent 2 (harness + uninstall) — ✅ Verified byte-for-byte: harness line 257,
  the finally-block guard proving the harness can't cause early exit, the
  System32 PowerShell 5.1 spawn, and the .hide close policy. The soak-exit story
  (your window close → hide → graceful quit within 1 second) is now
  mechanism-complete.

  Agent 3 (SDK docs) — ✅ Verified via GitHub: 0.5.4 is the newest SDK release;
  no upstream fix exists; no official input-timing or coalescing mechanism — the
  fix is ours to make.

  My own net-new finding: the patched renderer's static-prefix path replays
  every dynamic command against every damage region (N×M ≈ 10k clipped
  replays/frame worst case), and the sparse-damage path silently degrades to
  union raster outside a narrow window (2–8 rects, <75% union coverage). Five
  concrete instrumentable candidates are now enumerated in the plan for the
  reproduction step to discriminate.

  The Opus fix-design agent is still working; when it reports I'll compare its
  design against these five candidates, finalize the plan, and present it for
  your approval.

✻ Worked for 4m 45s
● Agent "Design scroll-lag fix" finished · 12m 43s

● The Opus design agent converged with my independent analysis — no
  contradictions, and it added two sharp refinements: the 60-second growth is
  chart rasterization weight (warmed area-fills), gated by chartDataEqual
  invalidation, and scroll frames are inherently full-viewport, so the app alone
  can't make scrolling cheap — the definitive fix is input scheduling (process
  input before present + coalesce scroll) in the runtime via the pinned patch
  flow. Let me pull its complete implementation plan before finalizing.

     edit
     SD-300 v3: Fix blockers, drive to release, merge to main

     Context

     Codex ran the SD-300 v3 native-GUI effort for 24+ hours and paused with two
     handoffs
     (docs/agents/handoff/2026-07-21-001-...md, 2026-07-22-002-...md).
     Implementation is
     substantially complete and pushed (8aaeef7, draft PR #4, exact-head
     ordinary CI green
     on all six targets). Operator direction this session (Emmett, 2026-07-22):

     - Drive to release. Get it functional, published, and merged back to main.
     Perfection is not the bar; patches can follow.
     - Soak deprioritized. The 2-hour soak is NOT required pre-release — it
     becomes a
     post-release backlog task for Codex (overnight capable).
     - Makira ships. Operator purchased the font (Craftwork Design; no license
     file was
     shipped with the purchase, only font files). Operator explicitly directed:
     ship it.
     Record as an operator-authorized waiver on the board with date +
     attribution.
     - Soak early-exit: resolved. Operator confirms he probably opened/closed
     the GUI
     window around 00:03 — combined with code findings (SDK close_policy = .hide
     +
     shouldQuitForHiddenWindow tick with tray off ⇒ graceful quit; no
     watchdog/self-exit
     exists), the exit is attributed to operator interaction, not a product
     defect.

     Root-cause findings (investigation phase, 3 Explore agents + local 
     evidence)

     Scroll lag (~1 min warm) — THE remaining functional defect

     - 60-sample history buffers (gui/src/main.zig:28,107-116,1030-1038) start
     zero-initialized; as they fill over ~60 s, the charts' per-tick dirty
     region grows to
     full-width (charts gui/src/app.native:105-291, esp. Network :269).
     - Amplified by unkeyed-command full-repaint escalation and the 8-dirty-rect
     cap
     (SDK patch canvas_frame.zig:1034-1097, serialization.zig:1039) — after
     warm-up,
     every 1-second tick escalates toward near-full repaint.
     - Input events and the 1-second data tick share ONE single-threaded
     update() queue
     (engine worker publishes latest-only snapshots
     gui-engine/src/lib.rs:515-934; GUI
     pulls on a 1 s repeating UI-thread timer main.zig:576-587) — repaint cost
     starves
     scroll/input handling.
     - Ruled out: render memo growth, per-tick alloc churn, process
     reconciliation.
     - Instrumentation: no app-level logging in release (-Dtrace=off); latency
     measurable
     via SD300_RENDER_BENCH headless bench (gui/src/tests.zig:598-759).
     - INDEPENDENTLY VERIFIED (Fable, direct code read):
       - pushHistory = shift-left of all 60 samples per tick
     (main.zig:1030-1038) —
     zero-filled slots make early damage tiny; filled buffers change all 60
     values
     per second. Confirms the ~60 s onset mechanism.
       - Charts on 7 sections (Overview/CPU/Memory/Disk/GPU/Network/Thermals,
     app.native:105-291); Processes/Drivers have none — but the warmed 15-min
     Processes run measured 1.58% avg CPU, so warmed idle ticks are cheap;
     severe lag
     must come from scroll-frame cost interacting with warmed damage state.
       - Dirty-rect cap = 8 (serialization.zig:1039); sparse multi-region path
     engages
     only for 2..8 rects with area/union < 0.75
     (reference_damage_union_fallback_ratio = 0.75, patch) — otherwise union
     raster.
       - Static-prefix path replays EVERY dynamic command against EVERY damage
     region
     (N×M replay, patch renderPassDamageWithStaticPrefix) — ~1.3k commands × up
     to
     8 regions; scroll may also break the fill_rect-only prefix match /
     fingerprint,
     forcing full prefix re-raster per scroll position.
       - Reproduction must distinguish: (a) chart-fill damage growth, (b) N×M
     command
     replay, (c) union-fallback raster, (d) scroll-position–dependent prefix
     misses,
     (e) input-queue backlog. All five are now concretely instrumentable
     targets.

     Soak exit (code 0 @ 00:03:52) — attributed, closed

     - Quit paths: authenticated quit event Local\SD300.Gui.Quit.v1
     (gui/src/platform/window_visibility.zig:18,235-251, main.zig:1374) used by
     updater/uninstaller; default close → hidden → shouldQuitForHiddenWindow
     (main.zig:388-397,572-574,1406). No watchdog, no idle timer.
     - Timeline: Honk300 MSI acceptance ran 23:56 (unrelated); operator at
     machine ~00:03;
     operator confirms probable close. Post-release soak rerun goes to Codex
     backlog.

     Native SDK research (docs + GitHub changelog, v0.4.0–v0.5.4)

     - Locked 0.5.4 is the CURRENT latest SDK release — no upstream fixes to
     backport.
     (Fable-verified via GitHub releases: v0.5.4 newest, July 21; SDK ships
     ~2-3×/week
     so recheck at release time.)
     - No documented input/frame-timing hook, message-priority, or coalescing
     mechanism
     exists in the SDK — input starvation under update floods is unmitigated
     upstream;
     any fix is ours (app-side or reviewed-patch-side).
     - Zig 0.16.0 x86_64-Linux codegen bug already handled in 0.5.4 (forced LLVM
     backend).

     Uninstall fix (uncommitted 2-file diff) — REVIEWED, sound; land as staged

     - Hosted failure: Remove-Item (no -Recurse) on nonempty receipt parent
     prompts;
     noninteractive Windows PowerShell fails; rollback worked correctly.
     - Staged fix: [IO.Directory]::Delete(parent,$false), tolerate only Win32
     145
     (ERROR_DIR_NOT_EMPTY), rethrow everything else + validation fixture
     (unrelated
     sibling byte-preservation).
     - Independent review findings (Explore agent, full-code):
       - Cleanup string executes under real Windows PowerShell 5.1
     (trusted_windows_system_executable → System32 powershell.exe,
     src/update.rs:3272-3285); validation script runs under pwsh 7 in CI but
     transitively exercises the 5.1 runtime via & $Binary uninstall --json.
       - Every construct (typed catch, HResult, -band, [IO.Directory]::Delete)
     behaves identically on .NET Framework/5.1 and PS 7. No compatibility gap.
       - Failure propagation intact: non-tolerated error → powershell exits
     nonzero →
     run_status Err → WindowsUninstallImageHandoff::finish rollback ("installed
     executable was restored"). Fail-closed contract preserved.
       - The diff mirrors the already-reviewed native pattern
     remove_empty_managed_receipt_directory (src/update.rs:3229-3248).
       - Receipt parent = %LOCALAPPDATA%\sd300 (dedicated app dir).
     - Three Terra hardening questions → resolve as: KEEP staged diff unchanged;
     BACKLOG the hardening (HRESULT facility/full-equality guard, File.Delete
     receipt
     removal, forced child CWD outside receipt tree). Rationale: false-tolerate
     risk is
     conservative (leaves a dir behind); scope expansion now adds risk in a
     drive-to-release session; hosted CI is the authoritative 5.1 proof either
     way.

     Execution model (operator-directed, 2026-07-22)

     - Fable (this session) = orchestrator. Owns investigation, planning,
     decisions,
     root-cause logic, review of all diffs, board maintenance, and final
     verification
     judgments. Conserves context by delegating mechanical/large edits.
     - Implementation agents = Opus 4.8 [1m] at xhigh effort. Once Fable is
     confident
     in a change, an Opus agent executes it with a precise change spec (files,
     exact
     edits, constraints, validation commands to run). One focused change per
     agent;
     results reviewed by Fable before commit.
     - Fable codes directly only when a change is small enough that delegating
     costs
     more than doing (tiny edits, board files, commit assembly) or when an
     implementer
     stalls and direct intervention is faster.
     - Long-running validation (builds, test suites, hosted CI) runs in
     background; Fable
     reviews outcomes, never idles on them.

     Step 0.5 — Write the ADRs (operator-required, for future Codex agents)

     Create docs/adr/ (family convention: Goose docs/adr/0031-...) and record
     the
     session's findings, reasoning, and decisions so any future agent can trace
     how each
     blocker was cleared without re-deriving it:
     - ADR 0001 — Soak early-exit attribution: evidence chain (event logs,
     harness
     finally-block guard, .hide close policy + shouldQuitForHiddenWindow
     mechanism,
     operator confirmation); decision: no product defect; unattended-rerun
     discipline
     (quarantine the machine, exit-attribution logging) for the backlog soak.
     - ADR 0002 — Warmed-state scroll latency: verified root-cause chain
     (shift-left pushHistory → chartDataEqual invalidation → 8-rect cap / 0.75
     union fallback → full-view N×M replays → input starvation on the shared
     update
     queue); measurement-first fix ladder (bench extension → app-side
     visible-page
     scoping → runtime input-before-present/scroll-coalescing via pinned patch);
     rejected alternatives (raising the rect cap; slowing cadence) and why.
     - ADR 0003 — Managed-uninstall receipt-parent cleanup: prompt root cause,
     the empty-only Directory.Delete model, PS 5.1/7 runtime analysis, why the
     three hardening questions were deferred (conservative false-tolerate, scope
     discipline), hosted CI as authoritative proof.
     - ADR 0004 — v3.0.0 release-scope decisions: operator-directed soak
     deferral,

     provided),
     #giu deferred to v3.1.0 (update-transaction risk isolation), evidence-class

     dropped).
     ADRs are committed with the work they describe (0001/0002 with the fix
     commits,
     0003 with the uninstall commit, 0004 with the release commit) and
     cross-referenced
     from the task detail files and both changelogs per the lockstep rule.

     Step 1 — Land the managed-uninstall fix (unblocks Windows qualification 
     lane)

     1. Independent review of the two-file diff; resolve the three hardening
     questions
     conservatively (likely: keep low-word check but verify facility from the
     real 5.1
     runtime; prefer minimal change that passes hosted proof; do not expand

     scripts\validate-windows-self-update.ps1)),

     (includes
     the two local docs commits).
     4. Dispatch exact-head Windows Native Installers qualification; require the
     unrelated-sibling preservation proof to pass.

     Step 2 — Fix the scroll lag (the functional defect)

     Converged design (Opus Plan agent + Fable independent analysis, mutually
     verified):

     Root cause (validated in code): required per-second repaints — full-width
     warmed
     chart invalidation (chartDataEqual + shift-left pushHistory) plus live
     table
     churn — escalate off the sparse damage path (8-rect cap
     serialization.zig:1039,
     0.75 union-fallback ratio) into full-view/N×M command replays that starve
     scroll
     input on the single-threaded update() loop (main.zig:385-522,576-587).
     Scroll
     frames are inherently full-viewport, so app-side work alone cannot make
     scrolling
     cheap — input scheduling is the definitive lever.

     Sequenced fix (stop at the first level that passes the warmed-scroll test):
     1. MEASURE first: extend the SD300_RENDER_BENCH headless bench
     (gui/src/tests.zig:598-759) with warmed histories + sections 5/6/8 +
     isolated scroll frames, run -Dtrace=off; attribute p95 by damage mode
     (.damage vs .fallback + dirty ratio); plus a physical warmed-scroll repro
     on the exact bundle. Chart invalidation gate: chartDataEqual
     (chart.zig:233-254 in the staged SDK).
     2. App-side data-tick scoping in gui/src/main.zig (LOW RISK, ship first):
     scope per-tick model mutation/invalidation to what the active section
     actually
     renders (histories keep collecting — presentation binding only), so warmed
     ticks stop invalidating charts/tables the current view does not show.
     3. If warmed scroll still stalls: runtime scheduler fix through the pinned
     patch
     (scroll-event coalescing + process-input-before-present in the Windows host
     +
     canvas_frame.zig — both already-pinned files), with lockstep SHA-256 pin
     updates through both patch preparers (prepare-native-sdk.ps1:18,45-82,
     .mjs:20,23-33) and the distribution verifiers.
     Rejected: raising the 8-dirty-rect cap (touches unpinned serialization
     files,
     risks CPU-budget regression from more sparse-region replays).
     Regression test: add an env-gated warmed-state bench assertion in
     tests.zig.
     Constraints: one-second visible cadence contractual; no collector fidelity
     reduction; hidden-mode behavior unchanged; strict checks + native tests +
     engine
     self-test + no CPU-budget regression (short bounded foreground sample).
     Verify: same warmed-scroll procedure that reproduced the lag + bench
     numbers;
     input-p95 <=50 ms on the bounded sample.

     Step 3 — Rebuild + focused performance/functional acceptance (not the 2-h 
     soak)

     Operator requirement (2026-07-22): explicitly prove the app is not a
     performance
     hog before release. Baseline already proven on the pre-fix release build:
     15-min
     foreground 1.58% of one logical core (≤2% budget), 30-min hidden 0.18%
     (≤1%),
     memory ~85 MiB working set / ~207-227 MiB private (≤150/≤300 budgets), no
     growth.
     Because the fix touches the render path, re-prove on the fixed build:
     - Fresh release-shaped build through the distribution-locked wrapper;
     strict
     checks, native tests, engine self-test.
     - 15-min foreground Processes run (must stay ≤2% CPU, memory within
     budgets).
     - Bounded hidden-mode sample (must stay ≤1%).
     - Physical smoke: all sections, both modes, scroll after 2+ min warm (the
     original
     defect procedure), close/tray behavior, export.
     - No measurement observers attached during perf sampling (proven
     contamination).
     Full 2-h soak + formal frame/input p95 evidence → Codex backlog
     (post-release).

     Step 4 — Qualification for release

     - Exact-head CI green (ordinary CI, six GUI targets, release planning,
     macOS
     preflight, Windows Native Installers incl. new uninstall proof).
     - Local release checks per AGENTS.md step 5 (fmt/clippy/test/build/publish
     dry-run,
     dist plan, npm --prefix gui ci|check|test, product-version check).

     Step 5 — Release execution (operator-authorized this session)

     1. Mark PR #4 ready; merge to main via a normal PR merge commit — verified
     pattern:
     v2 landed as "Merge pull request #3 from QubeTX/codex/sd300-v2-release".
     2. Main-branch release workflow runs: cargo-dist artifacts, draft v3.0.0,
     managed
     wrappers/routers, Windows MSI/EXE lanes, macOS PKG, six GUI targets,
     candidate
     lifecycle qualification, SBOM/attestations, crate publish + latest
     promotion.
     3. Monitor workflow; repair forward only (no retag/force-push/byte
     replacement).
     4. Post-public verification: fresh public-byte checks, cargo install 
     tr300-tui,
     stable installer URLs, website command surfaces, physical Windows install
     of the
     public MSI + sd300 gui smoke.

     Step 6 — Phase 2 of this session: #giu as a follow-up feature release

     Operator direction (2026-07-22): ship #giu with the main release if easy,
     otherwise
     as a patch release immediately after — "I want you to do that as well."
     Decision: NOT in v3.0.0 — #giu modifies the update transaction surface and
     carves a
     deliberate exception to "install/update never launches the app"
     (coordinator
     relaunch after success). Bundling it would destabilize the main release.
     Plan: after v3.0.0 is verified public, design + implement #giu in this
     session as
     v3.1.0 (version per repo changelog convention) on a fresh codex/ branch:
     tray/settings "Update now" surface → verified coordinator launches the
     existing
     owner-preserving CLI update transaction → GUI exits via the authenticated
     endpoint
     as today → coordinator relaunches the GUI only after verified success;
     failure
     paths surface honestly (no silent retry loops); Linux behavior explicit (no
     tray).
     Same qualification + release automation path as v3.0.0. Move #giu from
     To-Do to
     Active when Phase 2 starts; it keeps (needs #qv3) satisfied by then.

     Step 7 — Backlog population (operator asked: "what needs to be in the 
     backlog?")

     Create/move these as Backlog tasks for Codex (post-release):
     - Two-hour Processes soak + frame-p95/input-p95 full evidence on the
     released bytes
     (unattended, machine quarantined, exit-attribution aware).
     - Published-v2-binary PTY replay on hosted targets (open nsp verification).
     - Physical macOS acceptance when hardware access returns.
     - Claude review workflow repair (external tool failure).
     - Screen-reader/accessibility exposure beyond named canvas.
     - Makira license-evidence follow-up (obtain embedding license doc from
     vendor) —
     reduces future release risk; operator-authorized ship stands for v3.0.0.
     - Uninstall-fix hardening trio (HRESULT facility guard, File.Delete receipt
     removal, forced child CWD outside receipt tree).
     - Board hygiene: archive Done v2 tasks into milestone detail.

     Verification (end-to-end)

     - Scroll lag: warmed-state physical scroll test passes; bounded latency
     sample within
     budget; no cadence loss (1 s updates still visible).
     - Uninstall: hosted Windows qualification green including sibling
     preservation.
     - Release: workflow fully green; public bytes verified; cargo install 
     tr300-tui --version 3.0.0 installs working sd300; installed GUI launches
     via sd300 gui.
     - Board: TASKS.md reflects reality; waivers dated/attributed; backlog
     populated;
     milestone #v3n closes only when its in-scope children complete.

     Constraints carried from repo docs (do not violate)

     - Bare sd300 chooser unchanged; sd300 gui only additive command.
     - Six-target contract unchanged; no new owned targets.
     - One-second foreground data contract; no unbounded queues.
     - Receipt parents never recursively deleted; fail-closed ownership.
     - No retag/force-push/byte replacement; changelog lockstep rule (both
     files, same
     commit); distribution locks (SDK 0.5.4/Zig 0.16.0, hash-pinned patch flow).

      73 +## Addendum (2026-07-22): the Windows PowerShell 5.1 `-Command`
         + exit-code contract                                            
      74 +                                                               
      75 +The first hosted qualification of this fix still failed: the ch
         +ild                                                            
      76 +powershell.exe exited 1 even though the sibling was preserved c
         +orrectly. Local                                                
      77 +reproduction on real Windows PowerShell 5.1 proved the mechanis
         +m:                                                             
      78 +`powershell.exe -Command "<string>"` mirrors the FINAL statemen
         +t's `$?` in its                                                
      79 +exit code. A caught-and-swallowed terminating error (our tolera
         +ted                                                            
      80 +nonempty-parent catch) — and equally a failed `-ErrorAction Sil
         +entlyContinue`                                                 
      81 +cmdlet — leaves `$?` false, so a tolerated outcome in last posi
         +tion reports a                                                 
      82 +false failure. PowerShell 7 does not behave this way, which is 
         +why the earlier                                                
      83 +PS7-only local proof missed it (and why hosted 5.1 remains the 
         +authoritative                                                  
      84 +gate).                                                         
      85 +                                                               
      86 +Correction: the composed cleanup string now ends with a termina
         +l `exit 0`.                                                    
      87 +Empirically verified on 5.1: tolerated outcomes exit 0 with the
         + sibling                                                       
      88 +byte-intact; an uncaught `throw` aborts execution before reachi
         +ng the marker                                                  
      89 +and still exits 1 (fail-closed preserved); the empty-parent cas
         +e still removes                                                
      90 +the directory and exits 0. This also removes the latent same-sh
         +ape hazard for                                                 
      91 +whichever suppressed removal happens to be the string's last st
         +atement.                                                       
      92 +                                                               
      93 +Lesson recorded for future agents: when validating updater Powe
         +rShell behavior,                                               
      94 +the exception semantics AND the host exit-code contract are sep
         +arate claims, and                                              
      95 +each must be proven on Windows PowerShell 5.1 specifically.    
      96 +

          -root siblings;

● All three lanes are now running against the same head (1c103c1) —
  qualification, Release, and CI — which is the alignment we want for the merge
  candidate.

  Where things stand after this eventful stretch:

  The uninstall lane needed two fixes, not one. The original prompt bug was
  fixed as staged, but hosted qualification exposed a second, hidden defect:
  Windows PowerShell 5.1's -Command exit code mirrors the last statement's $?,
  so the correctly tolerated nonempty-parent outcome still reported failure.
  Codex's PS7-only local proof structurally couldn't catch this — I reproduced
  it on real 5.1 locally in one attempt, verified the fix shape empirically
  (tolerated → 0, uncaught throw → 1, empty dir still removed), landed it with
  an ADR 0003 addendum, and re-dispatched qualification. This whole episode is
  prime post-mortem material: exception semantics and host exit-code contracts 
  are separate claims, each needing proof on the authoritative runtime.

  In flight now: the qualification run (watched, with real conclusion reporting
  this time), Release + CI at the same head, the warmed-state bench (scroll-lag
  measurement), and your three requested review agents — engine/ABI + readings,
  Native SDK GUI, and updater/installer (that third one now includes an
  over/under-engineering sweep per your note about loop side-effects).

  One process correction on my side: the exit-code commit accidentally swept in
  the bench agent's in-progress test scaffolding via git add -A — it's additive
  declaration-only test code (harmless, superseded by the agent's final
  version), but I've switched to explicit staging while implementers share the
  tree.

● While the full report comes back, the fix shape is already clarifying: if
  scroll frames are inherently full-viewport at 16–22 ms of software raster, the
  lever isn't making them cheaper — it's making sure a wheel burst costs one
  frame at the latest offset instead of one frame per event (scroll coalescing),
  with pending input processed before each expensive raster starts
  (input-before-present). That's exactly the level-3 runtime fix the design
  agent pre-planned through the pinned patch flow. The decision gets made on the
  full numbers.

  three CI lanes at 1c103c1.

      48  - Fixed an uninstall failure that could occur when the program'
          s small settings folder also held a person's own unrelated file
          s. Uninstall now removes only its own records, leaves everythin
          g else byte-for-byte untouched, and removes the folder itself o
          nly when it is completely empty. Our automated checks now prove
           an unrelated file placed next to the program's records survive
          s uninstall exactly as it was.
      49  - Fixed a follow-on quirk where that same careful cleanup could
           report a failure even though it had done everything right, bec
          ause of how an older built-in Windows component signals success
          . Real problems still stop the process and restore the previous
           state.
      50  - Fixed updates and downloads failing on ordinary Windows compu
          ters that only have the built-in Windows automation shell and n
          ot the newer optional one. The program now finds the built-in s
          hell directly in its trusted system location, which also protec
          ts against a class of tampering.
      51 +- Fixed updates failing for people who start them from the newe
         +r optional Windows shell: settings inherited from it could conf
         +use the built-in shell the updater relies on. The updater now g
         +ives that built-in shell a clean start every time.             
      52  - Fixed the program leaving behind one small empty bookkeeping 
          folder after certain installation transitions, which could also
           prevent its settings folder from being fully removed at uninst
          all. Bookkeeping folders are now tidied up when their work is d
          one, and anything unrelated in them is always left alone.
      53  - Fixed the desktop app becoming very laggy to scroll after bei
          ng open for a minute or so. Fast mouse-wheel movement could pil
          e up faster than the app could redraw, and once the live charts
           filled with a full minute of history the pile-up became notice
          able. Wheel movement is now batched so scrolling stays smooth n
          o matter how long the app has been open.
      54  - Fixed a confusing situation where turning the tray icon on or
           off in settings and then closing the window could leave the ap
          p running invisibly with no way to bring it back (or quit it ev
          en though its tray icon was still there). Closing now behaves a
          ccording to how the app actually started, matching the "restart
           required" note in settings.

      3816      }
      3817  }
      3818  
      3819 +/// Inno uninstallers finish through a relaunched temp copy a
           +fter the original                                            
      3820 +/// process returns, and that copy occasionally loses the rac
           +e to remove the                                              
      3821 +/// original `unins*.exe` even though the registration, data 
           +file, and payloads                                           
      3822 +/// are gone. Wait briefly for the self-deletion; if a data-l
           +ess orphan                                                   
      3823 +/// remains inside the just-retired root, remove the proven-d
           +ead file and                                                 
      3824 +/// reclaim the directory only when empty. Best-effort: the u
           +ninstall itself                                              
      3825 +/// already succeeded and was verified, so failures here are 
           +not fatal.                                                   
      3826  #[cfg(windows)]
      3827 +fn reap_orphaned_inno_uninstaller(executable: &Path) {       
      3828 +    let data = executable.with_extension("dat");             
      3829 +    for _ in 0..50 {                                         
      3830 +        if !executable.is_file() {                           
      3831 +            return;                                          
      3832 +        }                                                    
      3833 +        if !data.is_file() {                                 
      3834 +            break;                                           
      3835 +        }                                                    
      3836 +        std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(1
           +00));                                                        
      3837 +    }                                                        
      3838 +    if executable.is_file() && !data.is_file() {             
      3839 +        let _ = std::fs::remove_file(executable);            
      3840 +        if let Some(root) = executable.parent() {            
      3841 +            let _ = std::fs::remove_dir(root);               
      3842 +        }                                                    
      3843 +    }                                                        
      3844 +}                                                            
      3845 +                                                             
      3846 +#[cfg(windows)]

● Update(HUMAN_CHANGELOG.md)
  ⎿  Added 1 line
      54  - Fixed the desktop app becoming very laggy to scroll after bei
          ng open for a minute or so. Fast mouse-wheel movement could pil
          e up faster than the app could redraw, and once the live charts
           filled with a full minute of history the pile-up became notice
          able. Wheel movement is now batched so scrolling stays smooth n
          o matter how long the app has been open.
      55  - Fixed a brief follow-up bug from that change where a single w
          heel click could fling the page all the way to the top or botto
          m. Each click now moves one steady, predictable step.
      56  - Fixed a subtle compatibility problem where installation recor
          ds written by the older built-in Windows shell carried an invis
          ible marker that made the program doubt its own installation. T
          he program now reads those records correctly no matter which sh
          ell wrote them.
      57 +- Extended that same fix to the records that prove a copy was i
         +nstalled through Rust's package manager, so the invisible marke
         +r can never make the program refuse an update there either.    
      58  - Fixed switching install methods occasionally leaving one dead
           leftover file from the previous installer behind. The switch n
          ow waits for the old installer to finish tidying itself up and 
          clears the leftover if it lingers.
      59  - Fixed uninstalling on fast computers sometimes reporting a fa
          ilure even though the uninstall was completing correctly — the 
          final tidy-up runs a moment after the main uninstall finishes, 
          and the program was checking too early. It now waits for that t
          idy-up. When an uninstall genuinely fails, the exact reason is 
          now reported instead of a bare error number.
      60  - Fixed a confusing situation where turning the tray icon on or
           off in settings and then closing the window could leave the ap
          p running invisibly with no way to bring it back (or quit it ev
          en though its tray icon was still there). Closing now behaves a
          ccording to how the app actually started, matching the "restart
           required" note in settings.

         +oduced none.

         +e only oracle for

         +rst** — print

         +le redirection,

● SD-300 v3.0.0 is published and verified. Here's where everything stands.

  The release is live

  Published 2026-07-22 15:02 UTC — 59 assets, crate on crates.io, all three
  attestations (provenance, SBOM, source identity) green.

  I verified the public bytes myself rather than trusting CI:

  - cargo install tr300-tui --version 3.0.0 --locked → installs a working sd300 
  3.0.0 with the gui command
  - Public Corporate MSI and GUI archive checksums both match their sidecars
  - gh attestation verify on the public MSI passes against the repository
  - Physical install on your Alienware: MSI installed, sd300 gui launched the
  app with live data (22 cores, 17% CPU, 26.7 of 31.5 GiB), snapshot exported 25
  capabilities, then sd300 uninstall removed everything — payload, process,
  shortcut — with no residue

  What it took

  Nineteen distinct defects, each root-caused and fixed: the scroll lag
  (full-viewport repaints colliding with warmed chart ticks on one queue), the
  wheel-momentum regression its own fix introduced, two GUI lifecycle bugs, and
  fifteen installer/updater/CI defects — a Windows PowerShell 5.1 exit-code
  contract, a probe that made stock Windows machines look like they had no
  shell, BOM-carrying receipts, an Inno self-deletion race, a UAC boundary
  swallowing errors, and the pre-publish tag refs that no draft can satisfy.

  The pattern that broke the 24-hour stall was simple: when a check failed 
  silently, I fixed the reporting before guessing. Six diagnostic commits, each
  answered in the next cycle.

  Waiting for you

  Two fixes are on main, unreleased — I stopped short of publishing a patch
  since you said you'd handle those. The sidebar showed a hardcoded v3.0.0 
  QUALIFICATION (now derived from the real version), and the post-publish crate
  poll used crates.io's web API, which now 403s anonymous clients (now uses the
  sparse index). A 3.0.1 bump would ship both and exercise the post-publication
  proof step, which is the one gate that has still never run.

  #giu (in-app updates) is planned as v3.1.0 and not started — it modifies the
  update transaction, so it deserves its own release rather than riding this
  one.

  The board carries six owned backlog tasks with the deferred evidence debt, and
  docs/agents/2026-07-22-codex-loop-post-mortem.md records why the effort
  stalled and the seven rules that closed it — cited at the top of AGENTS.md so
  Codex reads it on entry.

✻ Brewed for 10m 1s

  coordinator design clean. Work happens on a feature branch (pushes to main
  trigger releases, so the branch keeps iteration safe), then merge = release.

      269  
      270 +/// The desktop app's in-app update path: the identical proven
          +-owner update                                                 
      271 +/// transaction, followed by a GUI relaunch that the singleton
          + Open route makes                                             
      272 +/// idempotent. Relaunch requires both the explicit hidden coo
          +rdinator flag and                                             
      273 +/// a successful outcome, so installers and ordinary terminal 
          +updates never                                                 
      274 +/// launch the app.                                           
      275 +pub fn run_with_relaunch(json: bool, relaunch_gui: bool) -> Re
          +sult<i32> {                                                   
      276 +    let exit_code = run(json)?;                               
      277 +    if should_relaunch_gui(relaunch_gui, exit_code) {         
      278 +        // Relaunch problems report on stderr only; the update
          +'s stdout                                                     
      279 +        // contract and exit code stay exactly what the transa
          +ction produced.                                               
      280 +        let _ = crate::gui::launch();                         
      281 +    }                                                         
      282 +    Ok(exit_code)                                             
      283 +}                                                             
      284 +                                                              
      285 +fn should_relaunch_gui(requested: bool, exit_code: i32) -> boo
          +l {                                                           
      286 +    requested && exit_code == 0                               
      287 +}                                                             
      288 +

      291          Ok(installation) => installation,

      416      false

      418  
      419 +/// Start the installed CLI's own update transaction as a deta
          +ched coordinator                                              
      420 +/// on behalf of the desktop app. The CLI stays the sole lifec
          +ycle engine; this                                             
      421 +/// only resolves a proven absolute product location — never a
          + PATH lookup —                                                
      422 +/// and hands it `update --json --relaunch-gui`, so a successf
          +ul transaction                                                
      423 +/// reopens the app through the idempotent singleton Open rout
          +e. Output streams                                             
      424 +/// to a log beside the settings so failures stay inspectable 
          +after the GUI                                                 
      425 +/// exits mid-update.                                         
      426 +pub fn spawn_update_coordinator() -> std::result::Result<PathB
          +uf, String> {                                                 
      427 +    let cli = locate_update_cli().ok_or_else(|| {             
      428 +        "the SD-300 CLI was not found at a proven install loca
          +tion; run `sd300 update` from a terminal to repair the install
          +ation".to_string()                                            
      429 +    })?;                                                      
      430 +    let mut command = Command::new(&cli);                     
      431 +    command                                                   
      432 +        .args(["update", "--json", "--relaunch-gui"])         
      433 +        .stdin(Stdio::null());                                
      434 +    match update_launch_log() {                               
      435 +        Some((stdout, stderr)) => {                           
      436 +            command.stdout(stdout).stderr(stderr);            
      437 +        }                                                     
      438 +        // An unwritable log must never block the update itsel
          +f.                                                            
      439 +        None => {                                             
      440 +            command.stdout(Stdio::null()).stderr(Stdio::null()
          +);                                                            
      441 +        }                                                     
      442 +    }                                                         
      443 +    #[cfg(windows)]                                           
      444 +    command.creation_flags(CREATE_NO_WINDOW);                 
      445 +    #[cfg(unix)]                                              
      446 +    std::os::unix::process::CommandExt::process_group(&mut com
          +mand, 0);                                                     
      447 +    command                                                   
      448 +        .spawn()                                              
      449 +        .map(|_| cli.clone())                                 
      450 +        .map_err(|error| format!("could not start the updater 
          +at '{}': {error}", cli.display()))                            
      451 +}                                                             
      452 +                                                              
      453 +fn locate_update_cli() -> Option<PathBuf> {                   
      454 +    let current = std::env::current_exe().ok();               
      455 +    update_cli_from(current.as_deref(), crate::update::managed
          +_cli_binary())                                                
      456 +}                                                             
      457 +                                                              
      458 +fn update_cli_from(gui_exe: Option<&Path>, managed: Option<Pat
          +hBuf>) -> Option<PathBuf> {                                   
      459 +    let cli_name = if cfg!(windows) { "sd300.exe" } else { "sd
          +300" };                                                       
      460 +    let mut candidates = Vec::new();                          
      461 +    if let Some(dir) = gui_exe.and_then(Path::parent) {       
      462 +        // The composite root pairs `<root>/app/sd300-gui` wit
          +h `<root>/bin/sd300`.                                         
      463 +        if let Some(root) = dir.parent() {                    
      464 +            candidates.push(root.join("bin").join(cli_name)); 
      465 +        }                                                     
      466 +        // Linux managed installs share one flat `bin/` direct
          +ory.                                                          
      467 +        candidates.push(dir.join(cli_name));                  
      468 +    }                                                         
      469 +    candidates.extend(managed);                               
      470 +    #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]                               
      471 +    candidates.push(PathBuf::from("/usr/local/bin/sd300"));   
      472 +    candidates.into_iter().find(|path| path.is_file())        
      473 +}                                                             
      474 +                                                              
      475 +fn update_launch_log() -> Option<(Stdio, Stdio)> {            
      476 +    let path = crate::settings::settings_path()               
      477 +        .ok()?                                                
      478 +        .parent()?                                            
      479 +        .join("update-launch.log");                           
      480 +    if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {                     
      481 +        std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).ok()?;                
      482 +    }                                                         
      483 +    let file = std::fs::File::create(path).ok()?;             
      484 +    let clone = file.try_clone().ok()?;                       
      485 +    Some((Stdio::from(clone), Stdio::from(file)))             
      486 +}                                                             
      487 +                                                              
      488  fn spawn(candidate: &Path) -> std::io::Result<()> {
      489      #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
      490      let mut command = {

      413 +    pub const UpdateRequest = struct {                        
      414 +        ok: bool,                                             
      415 +        message: []const u8,                                  
      416 +    };                                                        
      417 +                                                              
      418 +    /// Ask the engine to spawn the installed CLI's detached u
          +pdate                                                         
      419 +    /// coordinator. The status is authoritative; `message` (b
          +orrowed from                                                  
      420 +    /// `buffer`) carries the resolved CLI path or the failure
          + reason and is                                                
      421 +    /// empty when the engine's text did not fit the caller's 
          +buffer.                                                       
      422 +    pub fn requestUpdate(self: *Runtime, buffer: []u8) UpdateR
          +equest {                                                      
      423 +        var required: usize = 0;                              
      424 +        const status = self.request_update_fn(buffer.ptr, buff
          +er.len, &required);                                           
      425 +        const fit = required > 0 and required <= buffer.len;  
      426 +        return .{                                             
      427 +            .ok = status == status_ok,                        
      428 +            .message = if (fit) buffer[0 .. required - 1] else
          + buffer[0..0],                                                
      429 +        };                                                    
      430 +    }

      802 +/// Hand the update to the installed CLI's detached coordinato
          +r through the                                                 
      803 +/// engine. The GUI process never mutates the installation its
          +elf: the CLI                                                  
      804 +/// runs its proven owner-preserving transaction, closes this 
          +app through the                                               
      805 +/// authenticated quit endpoint mid-update, and relaunches it 
          +only after                                                    
      806 +/// success (an already-current product keeps the app open and
          + simply focuses                                               
      807 +/// it through the singleton Open route).                     
      808 +fn requestUpdateNow(model: *Model) void {                     
      809 +    const runtime = active_engine orelse {                    
      810 +        model.status_buffer.set("Update unavailable — the GUI 
          +engine companion is not loaded. Run `sd300 update` from a term
          +inal.");                                                      
      811 +        return;                                               
      812 +    };                                                        
      813 +    var message_buffer: [512]u8 = undefined;                  
      814 +    const request = runtime.requestUpdate(&message_buffer);   
      815 +    if (request.ok) {                                         
      816 +        model.status_buffer.set("Update started · the monitor 
          +will close while the update runs and reopen when it finishes."
          +);                                                            
      817 +        return;                                               
      818 +    }                                                         
      819 +    if (request.message.len > 0) {                            
      820 +        var scratch: [384]u8 = undefined;                     
      821 +        const message = std.fmt.bufPrint(&scratch, "Update cou
          +ld not start · {s}", .{request.message}) catch "Update could n
          +ot start. Run `sd300 update` from a terminal.";               
      822 +        model.status_buffer.set(message);                     
      823 +    } else {                                                  
      824 +        model.status_buffer.set("Update could not start. Run `
          +sd300 update` from a terminal.");                             
      825 +    }                                                         
      826 +}

       5  ## [Unreleased]

       7 +## [3.1.0] - 2026-07-22                                        
       8 +                                                               
       9 +### Added                                                      
      10 +                                                               
      11 +- Added safe in-app and tray-driven updates. The Settings page'
         +s "Update now"                                                 
      12 +  action and the tray's "Update SD-300" item ask the GUI engine
         + to start the                                                  
      13 +  installed CLI as a detached, windowless update coordinator ru
         +nning the                                                      
      14 +  existing owner-preserving transaction (`update --json --relau
         +nch-gui`). The                                                 
      15 +  CLI resolves only from proven absolute product locations — th
         +e composite                                                    
      16 +  root's `bin/` sibling of the app directory, the shared flat `
         +bin/` layout,                                                  
      17 +  the managed receipt's recorded binary, or the fixed macOS PKG
         + path — never                                                  
      18 +  from a PATH lookup. Coordinator output streams to `update-lau
         +nch.log`                                                       
      19 +  beside the GUI settings so failures stay inspectable after th
         +e app closes                                                   
      20 +  mid-update. The GUI process itself never mutates the installa
         +tion.                                                          
      21 +- Added the hidden `--relaunch-gui` flag on `sd300 update`, res
         +erved for the                                                  
      22 +  GUI coordinator: after a successful transaction the CLI reope
         +ns the                                                         
      23 +  installed monitor through the idempotent singleton Open route
         +, so an                                                        
      24 +  already-current product keeps the running app open and simply
         + focuses it.                                                   
      25 +  Failures never launch the app, ordinary terminal updates are 
         +unchanged, and                                                 
      26 +  the update's JSON stdout contract, help output, and exit code
         +s are                                                          
      27 +  byte-identical to v3.0.0.                                    
      28 +- Added the `sd300_engine_request_update` engine ABI entry back
         +ing the GUI                                                    
      29 +  action. The returned status is authoritative; the optional ca
         +ller buffer                                                    
      30 +  carries the resolved CLI path or the failure reason for the s
         +tatus line.                                                    
      31 +                                                               
      32 +### Changed                                                    
      33 +                                                               
      34 +- The Settings About badge now derives its version from the eng
         +ine's expected                                                 
      35 +  product version exactly like the sidebar label, and the produ
         +ct-version                                                     
      36 +  consistency check now treats literal markup versions as optio
         +nal (any                                                       
      37 +  literal that remains must still match the release) since visi
         +ble labels                                                     
      38 +  bind to the checked engine constant.                         
      39 +- The About panel's lifecycle copy now describes the in-app upd
         +ate handoff;                                                   
      40 +  installer ownership, elevation, rollback, and JSON contracts 
         +remain

       7  ---
       8  
       9 -## Not yet released — small corrections                        
       9 +## July 22, 2026 — update from inside the app                  
      10  
      11 +**Added**                                                      
      12 +                                                               
      13 +- You can now update SD-300 from inside the desktop app or from
         + its tray menu. One click runs the very same trusted update the
         + terminal command performs: the app closes while the update ins
         +talls, then reopens on its own once it succeeds. If you're alre
         +ady up to date, the app simply stays open.                     
      14 +- If an update can't start — for example when the installation 
         +is damaged — the app says so plainly and points you to the term
         +inal command, and a small log file records what happened so it 
         +can be diagnosed afterward. A failed update never leaves you wi
         +thout the working copy you already had.                        
      15 +                                                               
      16  **Fixed**
      17  
      18  - Fixed a leftover internal label in the app's sidebar that rea
          d "QUALIFICATION" next to the version. It now simply shows the 
          version the app is actually running.
      19  - Fixed an automated post-release check that reported a problem
           even when a release had published correctly, because the servi
          ce it asked has started turning away anonymous requests. It now
           asks the same source the package manager itself uses.
      20  
      21 +**Behind the scenes**                                          
      22 +                                                               
      23 +- The version shown on the app's About panel now always comes f
         +rom the running engine itself — the same arrangement the sideba
         +r already uses — so neither can ever drift out of date, and the
         + release checks were taught about that arrangement.            
      24 +- The About panel's wording now explains that the in-app update
         + hands the real work to the command-line tool, which stays in c
         +harge of installation ownership, elevation, and recovery; remov
         +ing SD-300 still happens from the terminal.                    
      25 +                                                               
      26  ---

> Are we pretty confident this one is going to go through correctly? If not,

● Short answer on confidence first, then the ADR work.

  Yes — I'm confident. The reasoning: every failure mode CI could catch has
  already been exercised locally on the same pinned toolchain (Rust 1.95, Zig
  0.16.0, SDK 0.5.4) — both crates' full test suites, all five non-Windows
  cross-target compiles (which is exactly where the new unix process_group and
  macOS candidate paths live), the strict model/markup check, the pinned release
  bundle whose self-test proves the new engine symbol resolves, and the
  byte-identity goldens for the CLI contract. No new dependencies (so the
  security audit can't flip), no installer-script changes (so the deeper Windows
  lanes in the release workflow run the same machinery that passed for v3.0.0
  yesterday). The one genuinely unprovable-before-merge risk is hosted-runner
  flakiness, which is retryable, not architectural.

  So I'll write the ADR now and push it onto the PR branch — the ADR convention
  here is that decisions ship with the work they describe, and restarting CI
  five minutes into a run costs little. Checking the current ADR series:

  Listed 1 directory (ctrl+o to expand)

● Audit complete — report and applied fixes

  Files found: 2. The root CLAUDE.md scored 88/100 (B+) — its architecture,
  dual-frontend model, and constraint docs are excellent after this session's
  updates, but it contained one factual defect. .tasks/CLAUDE.md scored 92/100 
  (A−) and needs nothing; it follows the tasks-memory skill's own governed shape
  and is current.

  The defect, now fixed in both CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md (kept in lockstep): the
  testing recipe claimed cargo test --locked runs "the gui-engine ABI layout
  assertions." It doesn't — Cargo.toml has [workspace] exclude = ["gui-engine"],
  so root tests never touch that crate. This isn't theoretical: earlier tonight
  the engine's topic-envelope version assertion sat un-run under the documented
  command and would have shipped stale if I hadn't tested the crate directly.
  Three targeted changes landed:

  1. Build & Test Commands now lists cd gui-engine && cargo test --locked with
  the workspace-exclusion warning.
  2. Recipe step 7 correctly states where each check lives: engine behavior
  tests in the excluded crate, #[repr(C)] layout assertions as Zig comptime
  checks.
  3. Testing quick reference adds the engine-crate line plus a note that
  gui-engine is not clippy-gated and its C-ABI not_unsafe_ptr_arg_deref findings
  are accepted pattern (pointing at ADR 0005 and Backlog #hrd) — so a
  contributor running clippy there doesn't think they broke the build.

  Both changelogs got the matching lockstep entries. All of this sits in the
  same held-locally docs batch, which pushes to main once v3.1.0's public bytes
  are verified.

  One tip from the skill worth knowing: mid-session you can press # to have
  learnings auto-incorporated into CLAUDE.md — handy for one-off gotchas so they
  don't wait for an audit pass.

  The release chain is still running on main; the monitor will report each stage
  as it concludes.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Root cause found — and it's expected, benign behavior, not a regression. The
  failing check is the Release workflow's immutability guard:

  ▎ Immutable tag v3.1.0 resolves to 2b4c9a5..., but this release source is 
  ▎ c894c15...

  Here's what's happening. In this repo, a push to main is the deploy trigger.
  The source-check job's first step refuses to let a published, immutable
  version tag be re-pointed at a different commit. My two post-release docs
  commits (0a1bf1f, c894c15) keep the version at 3.1.0 but are new commits — so
  the guard correctly fails loud (exit 1) and every deploy job skips. It's the
  workflow protecting the released bytes, exactly the documented "fails other
  partial-release states so a human can repair or bump forward" contract. The
  v3.1.0 release itself (2b4c9a5) was success; only the later same-version
  commits red.

  Crucially: no redeploy was attempted, and the public state is untouched (one
  v3.1.0 tag, release not draft, crate live). This is the first time main has
  received same-version commits after a release (v3.0.0→v3.1.0 happened on
  branches), which is why we're seeing it now.

  There's no clean "make main green without shipping" move — bumping the version
  and pushing would trigger a real 3.1.1 release, and editing the workflow is
  itself another same-version commit. The honest state is: main carries a red 
  Release check until the next deliberate version bump, which is by design. Let
  me record this so no future session mistakes it for breakage:

  Wrote 1 memory (ctrl+o to expand)

  One thing to flag — not a bug: the two post-release docs commits show a red
  Release check on main. That's the workflow's immutability guard refusing to
  re-point the published v3.1.0 tag at a new commit; all deploy jobs skip and
  the public release is untouched. It's the first time main has taken
  same-version commits after a release, and it clears itself on the next
  deliberate version bump. There's no clean way to green it without either
  shipping a release or adding yet another same-version commit.

  Two optional follow-ups, your call — I didn't do either autonomously since
  they're about your release hygiene:
  1. Document the guard in AGENTS.md so a future Codex agent doesn't panic at
  the red check (costs one more harmless red commit).
  2. Refine the workflow to skip cleanly instead of fail when a same-version
  main push is docs-only — a nicer long-term behavior, best folded into the next
  version bump so it doesn't red on its own.

● Now I fully understand it. The immutability check at lines 138–148 fires
  before the "already fully published → skip" decision, so it errors out before
  ever reaching the safe skip branch. The fix is surgical: compute the SHAs up
  front without failing, then apply the immutability guard only on the branches
  that would actually deploy (fresh release, or repair-hosting) — a
  fully-published version whose main has moved on is the benign case and must
  skip cleanly. This preserves every real protection.

● Root cause found, and it's a transient infrastructure flake, not my changes:
  the macOS producer failed at the sign/notarize step with
  libsd300_engine.dylib: A timestamp was expected but was not found. That's the
  classic symptom of Apple's timestamp server (timestamp.apple.com) being
  briefly unreachable during codesign — the signature gets created without a
  secure timestamp and fails verification. My version bump doesn't touch
  signing, and this same setup signed/notarized cleanly for v3.0.0 and v3.1.0.
  It's retryable, and it failed at signing before uploading, so no partial macOS
  asset is in the draft to conflict.

  Windows is still building; I'll re-run just the failed macOS job. Once both
  producers succeed, the qualify gate re-converges and publishes:

  transaction, relaunching only on success.
  - v3.1.1 — the release-workflow hygiene fix you asked for, plus the v3.1.0
  post-release docs (ADRs 0004/0005, README accuracy pass, gui-engine
  testing-doc correction).

  Both verified against public bytes: cargo install tr300-tui installs the right
  version, releases are promoted to latest, and gh attestation verify returns
  SLSA provenance bound to the exact release commits.

  The guard fix works — validated live. The refined source-check now skips
  cleanly on a post-release same-version main push instead of failing. I proved
  it in production: the final board push at published 3.1.1 went green with 
  every deploy job skipped and no re-deploy, where the equivalent pushes before
  the fix (0a1bf1f, c894c15) had failed. It's also documented in AGENTS.md as a
  warn-and-investigate note.

  Branches cleaned up — only main remains, locally and remotely; working tree
  clean and synced.

  Now the honest part. The 3.1.1 release was rougher than it should have been,
  entirely from mistakes I made and then had to recover:
  1. I pushed a board commit during the active release, splitting the branch
  head from the release draft's target commit.
  2. Because workflow_run-triggered producers get labeled with the branch head,
  I misread the release's legitimate producers as rogue and canceled two —
  leaving an incomplete draft.
  3. That split then failed the qualify gate's identity check (draft targets 
  eba3195, checkout is 4c612be).

  I recovered deterministically each time: deleted the mismatched draft and
  re-ran Release from the single current commit (4c612be) so all provenance is
  single-commit. A transient Apple timestamp-server flake failed the macOS
  signing once; a job re-run cleared it. I recorded all of it in operator memory
  — the core lessons being never push during a release and producers are 
  branch-head-associated, don't cancel them on that basis — so no future session
  repeats this.

  Net: the work you actually asked for is done and clean. The extra iterations
  cost time but never touched the already-public v3.1.0, and the final state is
  fully verified.

      221 +A self-inflicted release scare during the v3.1.1 patch, record

      222 +mistake is never repeated. Nothing public was ever corrupted; 
          +the cost was time                                             
      223 +and several avoidable rebuild cycles.                         
      224 +                                                              
      225 +**What happened.** While the v3.1.1 Release workflow was in fl
          +ight (draft built                                             
      226 +from commit `eba3195`), a one-line task-board commit (`4c612be
          +`) was pushed to                                              
      227 +`main`. That moved the branch head. Three things then went wro
          +ng in sequence:                                               
      228 +                                                              
      229 +1. The native-installer producers (`Windows Native Installers`
          +, `macOS Universal                                            
      230 +   Package`, `Linux Native GUI Packages`) trigger on `workflow
          +_run` of "Release"                                            
      231 +   and GitHub associates such runs with the **branch head at t
          +rigger time**, so                                             
      232 +   the real release's producers appeared in `gh run list` unde
          +r the *newer*                                                 
      233 +   `4c612be` SHA. They were misread as rogue duplicate runs an
          +d two were                                                    
      234 +   cancelled — leaving the draft missing its Windows and macOS
          + installers.                                                  
      235 +2. The qualify gate then failed its identity check: `release v
          +3.1.1 targets                                                 
      236 +   eba3195…, but this qualification checkout is 4c612be…`. The
          + draft's target                                               
      237 +   commit and the branch head had diverged, and the gate corre
          +ctly refuses to                                               
      238 +   publish a draft it can't tie to the checkout.              
      239 +3. A transient Apple timestamp-server flake (`libsd300_engine.
          +dylib: A timestamp                                            
      240 +   was expected but was not found`) failed one macOS signing a
          +ttempt on top of                                              
      241 +   the rest.                                                  
      242 +                                                              
      243 +**How it was handled.** Each step was root-caused from logs be
          +fore acting, not                                              
      244 +guessed. The deterministic recovery was to make every artifact
          + single-commit:                                               
      245 +`gh release delete v3.1.1 --yes` (a draft has no tag yet, so n
          +othing immutable                                              
      246 +is touched), then `gh run rerun <the 4c612be Release run>` so 
          +the draft target,                                             
      247 +the producers, and the qualify checkout all originate from the
          + current branch                                               
      248 +head `4c612be`. The macOS flake cleared with a plain job re-ru
          +n. Final proof:                                               
      249 +every asset's attestation resolves to `4c612be` (single-commit
          + provenance),                                                 
      250 +checksums match sidecars, the crate installs to `sd300 3.1.1`,
          + and the hosted                                               
      251 +producers exercised install/takeover/update/uninstall on all p
          +latforms.                                                     
      252 +                                                              
      253 +**How to avoid it — release-operations rules (now also in `AGE
          +NTS.md`):**                                                   
      254 +                                                              
      255 +- **Never push to `main` while a release is in flight.** Hold 
          +every push until                                              
      256 +  the version's tag is live. A second same-version push moves 
          +the branch head                                               
      257 +  and desynchronizes the whole `workflow_run` chain from the r
          +elease's commit.                                              
      258 +- **Producers/qualify are `workflow_run`-triggered and branch-
          +head-associated.**                                            
      259 +  A producer showing under a newer SHA than the release is nor
          +mal, not rogue.                                               
      260 +  Do **not** cancel a producer because of the SHA it displays.
      261 +- **Recovery is delete-draft + re-run-Release-from-one-commit*
          +*, never a partial                                            
      262 +  patch of a straddled draft. The `source-check` draft-exists 
          +guard requires the                                            
      263 +  old draft gone first.                                       
      264 +- **A single macOS signing failure with a timestamp error is a
          + transient Apple                                              
      265 +  infrastructure flake** — re-run the job once before treating
          + it as real; only                                             
      266 +  a repeat justifies adding a `codesign` timestamp retry.     
      267 +                                                              
      268 +The residual systemic fragility (that `workflow_run` consumers
          + checkout the                                                 
      269 +branch head rather than the triggering Release's commit) is ow
          +ned as hardening

          +d on top of a

      315  
      316 +**Release-operations discipline (learned the hard way in v3.1.
          +1; full account                                               
      317 +in the post-mortem addendum).** A release is a multi-workflow 
          +chain — `Release`                                             
      318 +builds the draft, then `workflow_run`-triggered producers (`Wi
          +ndows Native                                                  
      319 +Installers`, `macOS Universal Package`, `Linux Native GUI Pack
          +ages`) attach                                                 
      320 +native installers, then `Qualify and Publish Release` verifies
          + and publishes.                                               
      321 +                                                              
      322 +- **Never push to `main` while a release is in flight.** Hold 
          +every push until                                              
      323 +  the version's tag is live (`git ls-remote --tags origin | gr
          +ep vX`). A second                                             
      324 +  push moves the branch head and desynchronizes the whole chai
          +n from the                                                    
      325 +  release's commit, breaking the qualify identity check       
      326 +  (`release vX targets <A>, but this qualification checkout is
          + <B>`).                                                       
      327 +- **`workflow_run` producers are associated with the branch he
          +ad at trigger                                                 
      328 +  time, not the release's commit.** The real release's produce
          +rs can appear in                                              
      329 +  `gh run list` under a newer SHA. That is normal — do **not**
          + cancel a producer                                            
      330 +  because of the SHA it displays.                             
      331 +- **Recovery from a straddled/partial draft is deterministic:*
          +* `gh release                                                 
      332 +  delete vX --yes` (drafts carry no tag, so nothing immutable 
          +is touched), then                                             
      333 +  `gh run rerun <the Release run on the intended commit>` so t
          +he draft target,                                              
      334 +  producers, and qualify checkout all originate from one commi
          +t. Never patch a                                              
      335 +  straddled draft in place.                                   
      336 +- **One macOS signing failure with `A timestamp was expected b
          +ut was not found`                                             
      337 +  is a transient Apple timestamp-server flake** — re-run the j
          +ob once; only a                                               
      338 +  repeat justifies adding a `codesign` timestamp retry.       
      339 +                                                              
      340  Version tag pushes (`v*.*.*`) remain supported for explicit/ma
           nual releases, but the normal automation path is main-branch p
           ush. `CARGO_REGISTRY_TOKEN` must exist as a GitHub Actions sec
           ret; never commit registry tokens or publish from a local mach
           ine unless the user explicitly asks for an emergency manual pu
           blish after CI status has been checked.
      341  
      342  The package was moved to `tr300-tui` so the project can publis
           h while keeping the installed command and product identity as 
           `sd300` / SD-300. After release, verify `cargo install tr300-t
           ui --version {VERSION}` installs `sd300` and that the GitHub R
           elease assets are present.
